# Supplementary tables S1 to S32: per-configuration results under the Jensen and Gaussian wakes

All 32 tables of the Supplementary Material, converted from the LaTeX source with their printed numbers and captions. S1 to S19 and S30 to S32 are Jensen-wake tables, S20 to S29 are the detailed Gaussian-wake results. Power and wake-loss values are in kW, except where a caption states the objective scale. Colour highlighting of the PDF is not reproduced.

### Table S1

Wake loss and net expected power (wake-free ideal minus wake loss) at the largest configuration of each farm size, over feasible runs only, in kW (Supplementary S6). All columns are power quantities, not time-integrated energy. Amber rows have fewer than 15 feasible runs, so their statistics come from a favorable subset. Orange rows produced no feasible layout in any of the 30 runs.

### Table S2

Constraints inferred from the feasible layouts.

| Constraint | Value recovered | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Minimum inter-turbine spacing | 308.0 m | Smallest pairwise distance over all feasible layouts is 308.0000 m; no feasible layout falls below it. This equals 4 rotor diameters for a 77 m rotor. |
| Farm boundary | \|position\| ≤ R | Maximum boundary excess over all feasible layouts is exactly 0.0 m. |

### Table S3

Fidelity of each baseline to its original publication.

| Algorithm | Verdict | Deviation from the published form |
|---|---|---|
| GA [10] | faithful (variant) | Real-coded generational GA with binary tournament, one-point crossover and uniform-reset mutation; no elitism, which matches the simple GA. Per-gene mutation rate 0.1 is roughly 3x the usual 1/L heuristic. |
| DE [9] | faithful | DE/rand/1/bin with F = 0.5, CR = 0.9, both Storn and Price's recommended values. Distinct-index requirement and the jrand condition are correctly enforced. Mutant is clipped to the box before crossover, which is an added repair. |
| PSO [13] | deviates | Inertia-weight PSO with w = 0.7, c1 = c2 = 2.0 and no velocity clamping. Kennedy and Eberhart's c1 = c2 = 2 was specified with Vmax; the constant-inertia stability condition requires c1 + c2 < 2(1+w) = 3.4, and 4.0 exceeds it. Positions are clipped without resetting velocity. |
| GWO [11] | faithful (variant) | a, A, C, D and the three-leader average all match. Leaders are the current generation's top three rather than persistent best-so-far elites, so the alpha can degrade between iterations. |
| BBO [8] | faithful (variant) | Rank-based linear migration curves, per-SIV migration, roulette on emigration rate, elitism with keep = 2 — all canonical. Mutation uses a flat rate of 0.01 rather than Simon's species-count-derived m(S); duplicate clearing is absent. |
| SSA [14] | faithful (variant) | c1 schedule, leader move and follower chain are exact. The population is re-sorted by fitness every iteration, so leader and follower roles are reassigned each generation; canonical SSA sorts once and keeps fixed chain positions. |
| LX-SSA [15, 47] | faithful (variant) | Laplace coefficient drawn correctly from the inverse CDF. The operator is applied as a signed step toward the incumbent with one scalar coefficient shared across all dimensions, rather than the published component-wise two-parent form. Costs 2 evaluations per follower, which is why its budget is 6,030. |
| PF [12] | faithful (variant) | A genuine bootstrap particle filter: Gaussian propagation, Boltzmann importance weights on an elite quantile, systematic resampling. Operates on continuous coordinates rather than the grid of Eroglu and Seckiner, and the likelihood is normalized by the elite spread. Its four tuning parameters have no published reference values. |
